SSG Directives




5.2. Directive Descriptions
        The following alphabetically ordered subsections describe the directives in detail.

5.2.1. *ABORT
        The *ABORT directive causes SSG to terminate skeleton processing.

        Format
          *ABORT



        Description
        The *ABORT directive causes abnormal termination of the skeleton in execution.
        The line number and the source of the skeleton image (SKEL$ or define packet name)
        where the *ABORT was encountered are displayed. For example, if the *ABORT is
        encountered at line 25 while processing the main skeleton, the following message
        is displayed:

         **** ABORT ENCOUNTERED AT LINE 25 OF SKEL$

        At program termination, bit 27 of the condition word is set.

5.2.2. *ACCEPT
        The *ACCEPT directive allows SSG to function interactively.

        Format 1
           *ACCEPT(,options) variable-name val (val val...)

        Format 2
           *ACCEPT,S(options) val (val val...)

        Format 3
           *ACCEPT,L(options) label val (val val...)




5–4                                                                               7831 1784–010
                                                                                       SSG Directives




         Parameters
         options

                   B    Performs a PRINT followed by a READ rather than a TREAD.
                   E    Allows control images (images with @ as the first character) to be input.
                        If a control image is input, EOF$ will be set to 1 or 2.
                   L    Creates a stream generation statement (SGS) with the specified label.
                        The input becomes the fields and subfields.
                   N    Specifies that bracketed references on the input image are not to be
                        satisfied.
                   O    Solicits input from the operator console
                   S    Creates an SGS from the input


         variable-name

               The name of the string variable generated from the *ACCEPT input.

         label

               The label of the SGS generated from the *ACCEPT input.

         val

               Message printed before the read. The parameter val can be a string variable name,
               a numeric variable name, or a quoted string.

         Description
         The *ACCEPT directive allows the SSG skeleton to obtain input from you and from
         the operator.

              With no options, SSG creates a global string variable from the input.
              The O option sends the message text to the operator and allows the operator to
               provide the input.
              By using either the S or L options, you can create an SGS from the input.
                      If you specify the S option, your input is used to create the SGS label, fields,
                       and subfields. The label of the SGS is stored in the SSG global variable
                       ACCSGS$.
                      If you specify the L option, an SGS is created using the label specified on the
                       *ACCEPT directive, and your input becomes the fields and subfields.
                      The order in which the SGS is added to the chain of SGSs with the same label
                       is determined by the value of the reserved global variable SOPT$ (see 3.2.3.3).
                      If the value of the reserved global variable IOPT$ is nonzero, the SGS is
                       displayed in the SSG run log, preceded by the skeleton line number and a
                       colon ( : ).



7831 1784–010                                                                                         5–5
SSG Directives




            If you do not use the E option, any Exec control image ( @ ) input causes an error
             exit. This is also true if you use a “hard EOF” (non @EOF image) between the SSG
             call and actual skeleton execution.
                    If you use the E option and the control image is an @EOF (soft EOF), the global
                     variable EOF$ is set to 1. SSG can accept more input if the control image is a
                     “soft EOF.”
                    If you use the E option and the control image is a non @EOF (hard EOF), then
                     EOF$ is set to 2.
                    If there is no control image, then EOF$ is 0.
        Any bracketed references in the *ACCEPT input are satisfied if LBRK$ is clear and
        the N option is not specified. This means you can enter bracketed SGS and variable
        references on input.

        The maximum number of input characters for *ACCEPT is 121. If you use the L option,
        the label you specify is included as part of the 121-character limit.

        Example 1
            *ACCEPT,B name ‘What is your name?’

        Example 2
            *ACCEPT,OS ‘Enter boot tape SGS’

5.2.3. *ADD
        The *ADD directive performs dynamic expansion of the SGS chain.

        Format
            *ADD(,opts)        SGS(,options)     specification

        Parameters
        opts

                 A    Do not terminate in error if the source is not found or is not obtainable.
        options

             The same SGS options are available as on the SGS file identification statement.
             See Table 2–3 for a description of the options.

        specification

             The same four specification formats are available as on the SGS file identification
             statement. See 2.2 for a description of the four formats. For Formats 1 and 4 the
             filename can be an SDF file, a standard program file (PF), or a large program file
             (LPF). For Format 2 the filename can be a PF, an LPF, or a large element program
             file (LEPF). For Format 3 the filename can be a PF or an LPF.




5–6                                                                                    7831 1784–010
                                                                                 SSG Directives




         Description
         This directive allows you to dynamically add SGSs to the internal chain. All bracketed
         references are satisfied first. The order in which the SGSs are added to the chain of
         SGSs with the same label is determined by the value of reserved global variable
         SOPT$ (see 3.2.3.3). If the value of reserved global variable IOPT$ is nonzero, the SGSs
         are displayed in the SSG run log, preceded by the skeleton line number and a
         colon ( : ).

         An SGS label must be specified for specification formats 2, 3, or 4. In all cases, the
         label is from 1 to 12 characters from the set of alphabetics (A through Z and a through
         z), numerics (0 through 9), dollar sign ( $ ), and hyphen ( - ).

         Example 1
           *ADD SGS                     TEMP.SGSLIST

         Example 2
           *ADD SGS                     WORKFILE./ELEMENTS

         Example 3
           *ADD SGS                     DUMMY.

         Example 4
           *ADD SGS,ES                  SOMEFILE.ANELT      SI

5.2.4. *BRKPT
         The *BRKPT directive positions the generated output stream.

         Format
           *BRKPT(,options) (name)

         Parameters
         options
             Use any combination of characters. If LBRK$ is clear, bracketed references
             resolve to a string.

                K   List the generated output images following this breakpoint until the next
                    *BRKPT directive, or *EXIT, is encountered (see PRTOFF$, 3.2.3.2.
                P   Create the output file/element in output symbiont PRINT$ format. If
                    the P option is not present, output is generated in general symbolic
                    format. This option must be present if a page eject (*EJECT) is required
                    in the output file/element.
                R   Set the *BRKPT K option value to match the value on the SSG call line. If
                    the K option was present on the @SSG, the R option has the same
                    effect as the K option specified on *BRKPT.




7831 1784–010                                                                                   5–7
SSG Directives




        name
             Use any combination of characters. If LBRK$ is clear, use bracketed references.
             Can be a file or an element and must be in standard filename.eltname notation.
             For this SSG directive, only a space-period-space (.) terminates the scan of
             the image. The name specifies the destination of all generated output images
             after the *BRKPT directive until the next *BRKPT directive is encountered or you
             exit. If you omit name and the generated output stream needs maintenance,
             SSG assumes that there is an internal file.

        Description
        All generated output images encountered before the first *BRKPT directive in a
        skeleton are controlled by the presence or absence of the K option on the @SSG call.
        The images are sent to the file or element specified in param-3 of the @SSG call.

        The B option on the @SSG call and the variable ADD_GOS$ control the last part of the
        generated output stream. This last part is the collection of output images generated
        between the last-interpreted *BRKPT directive in the skeleton (or start of skeleton
        execution if no *BRKPT directive is encountered), and the exit. If the *BRKPT directive
        is not used, the B and K options control the entire generated output stream.

        Upon encountering a *BRKPT directive, SSG sets the values of ERRCNT$ and
        GOSCNT$ to 0; WARNINGCNT$ is not reset.

5.2.5. *CLEAR
        The *CLEAR directive creates and changes variable values.

        Format
            *CLEAR   variable-name

        Parameter
        variable-name

             Can be supplied by a string satisfying the description in 3.2.1 or a variable
             reference, process parameter reference, SGS reference, or set reference that
             returns such a string.

        Description
        SSG determines the variable to be cleared as follows:

            If a local variable of that name exists, SSG sets the value to zero.
            If no local variable of that name exists, SSG searches the global variable chain
             for that name, and if that name exists, sets it to 0.
            If SSG finds no global variable with that name, it creates one and gives it the
             value of 0. Once created, this global variable exists for the remainder of skeleton
             processing, unless it is removed (*REMOVE).




5–8                                                                                 7831 1784–010
                                                                                    SSG Directives




         Example 1
              *CLEAR   VAR

         Example 2
              *CLEAR   [VAR,1,1,1]

         Example 3
              *CLEAR   [#P]

5.2.6. *COPY
         The *COPY directive adds skeleton images from other files or elements into the
         SSG skeleton.

         Format
             *COPY(,options)     (file.)(element)

         Parameters
         options

                A   Do not terminate in error if the file or file.element is not found.
                N   Do not print the copied images.


         (file.)(element)

               Copy skeleton images from the file with this name.

         Description
         The *COPY directive allows you to copy in common skeleton images to help compose
         a main skeleton. This takes place on the first pass of the skeleton (skeleton
         verification, and so forth), so you cannot conditionally copy or put global variable
         references in *COPY statements.

              If the file or file.element does not exist, no copy takes place.
              If the A option is present and the source of the images is not found, you see a
               warning message but the skeleton is processed normally.
              If file is not specified, a search of SSG$PF, source-of-skel file, and TPF$ is made,
               in that order, until the reference is satisfied. The SSG$PF file must be assigned to
               the run in order for the search of that file to take place. If the element is not found,
               you see a warning message.
         Example 1
              *COPY,N TEMP.DEFINES

         Example 2
              *COPY,A TPF$.USER




7831 1784–010                                                                                      5–9
SSG Directives




        Example 3
          *COPY COPY-ELT

5.2.7. *CORRECT
        The *CORRECT directive merges temporary and permanent correction streams.

        Format
         *CORRECT(,options) element-name(/version-name) ;


                 ) (PCF) (WITH TCF) (GIVING PCF)
           PERM
         (
            TEMP

         (skeleton generated corrections)

         *ENDCORRECT

        Parameters
        options

            Can be supplied by a string, variable reference, process parameter reference,
            SGS reference, or set reference that returns a string. Valid values are

           none      In the absence of a (possibly assumed) GIVING phrase, the result
                     of the correct operation is directed to the generated output
                     stream.
           K         Used in conjunction with the P option or GIVING phrase to
                     additionally direct the images resulting from the correct operation
                     to the generated output stream.
           P         Operation of this option depends on PCFxxxx and PCFzzzz.
                     The P option is ignored if PCF is not a P-option PCF set.
                     If PCFzzzz is not specified, the P option forces SSG to assume
                     that a GIVING PCFxxxx phrase was specified.
                     If PCFzzzz is not specified and if PCFxxxx is not a P-option PCF
                     set, the assumption above is not made and the P option is
                     ignored.
                     If PCFzzzz is a P-option PCF set, the images resulting from the
                     merge are sent to the program file attached to PCFzzzz. A new
                     symbolic element is created (the old one, if it existed, is replaced)
                     with the same name as the element name (see 6.4).




5–10                                                                               7831 1784–010
                                                                               SSG Directives




         element-name/version-name

            Specifies the name of the element from which the correction streams are taken.
            Can be supplied by a string satisfying the description in 6.3 or by a variable
            reference, process parameter reference, SGS reference, or set reference that
            returns such a string.

         PCFxxxx

            Specifies the input permanent stream. Can be supplied by a string satisfying
            the description in 6.4 or by a variable reference, process parameter reference,
            SGS reference, or set reference that returns such a string.

               If this phrase is omitted, PCF is assumed.
               If TEMP is supplied, PCFxxxx is not allowed (and PCF is not assumed).
               If PCFxxxx is specified, it must be a defined PCF set.
         TCFyyyy

            Specifies the input temporary stream. Can be supplied by a string satisfying
            the description in 6.5 or by a variable reference, process parameter reference,
            SGS reference, or set reference that returns such a string.

               If this phrase is omitted, TCFxxxx is assumed and the TCF set corresponding
                to the selected PCF set is used.
               If PERM is supplied, TCFyyyy is not allowed (and no assumption is made).
               If TCFyyyy is specified, it must be a defined TCF set. Otherwise, a NO FIND
                condition results.
         PCFzzzz

            Specifies the output permanent stream. Can be supplied by a string satisfying the
            description in 6.4 or by a variable reference, process parameter reference, SGS
            reference, or set reference that returns such a string.

            If this phrase is omitted, and if the P option is specified on the *CORRECT directive
            and PCFxxxx is a P option PCF set, PCFxxxx is assumed. Otherwise, the results of
            the merge are always sent to the generated output stream.

         skeleton generated corrections

            Specifies the symbolic images generated as a result of interpreting the skeleton
            images delimited by the *CORRECT directive and matching *ENDCORRECT
            directive.




7831 1784–010                                                                                 5–11
SSG Directives




        Description
        All phrases are optional except element-name/version-name. SSG merges the
        specified correction streams with any skeleton-generated corrections.

             If you specify PERM, the input permanent corrections and skeleton-generated
              corrections are merged.
             If you specify TEMP, the input temporary corrections and skeleton-generated
              corrections are merged.
             If you specify neither PERM nor TEMP, input permanent corrections, input
              temporary corrections, and skeleton-generated corrections are merged.
        Skeleton-generated corrections are always used in the merge. If an element
        (correction stream) that is specified (or assumed) does not exist, the merge consists
        of the remaining specified (or assumed) entries.

        Skeleton-generated correction images never correct a PCF set or TCF set element
        entry, only a base symbolic element.

        Conflicts in line numbers on correction images are resolved as follows:

             TCF set corrections override PCF set corrections and skeleton corrections.
             PCF set corrections override skeleton corrections.
        Example
            @SSG,AE
            SKEL
            #ELT,UL     F.[P,1,1,1]
            *CORRECT      [P,1,1,1]
            -13,19
              A
              B
              C
            *ENDCORRECT
            @EOF
            PCF
            *DATA
            -10,11
              A12
              A13
              A14
            @EOF
              .
              .
              .




5–12                                                                              7831 1784–010
                                                                                 SSG Directives




         When you specify PERM on a *CORRECT directive, a revised temporary correction
         (RTC) entry is not created. No temporary stream is used in the merge. In addition,
         a created (*CREATE) element entry (primary TCF set only), has no correction stream
         attached to it when you create it; in this case, therefore, an RTC entry is not created.
         See 6.10 for more information about the RTC stream.

         The P option is ignored for an element entry that does not already exist in PCFzzzz
         (PCFxxxx if assumed). To create a symbolic element in an attached program file for
         an element that does not already exist as an element entry, specify a GIVING phrase
         on the *CORRECT,P directive. In the absence of the GIVING phrase, the P option is
         ignored for an element entry that does not already exist in PCFxxxx. When an element
         in an attached program file is revised and reinserted into the program file, an update
         process is simulated. In either case, placing a GIVING phrase on the *CORRECT,P
         directive ensures modification of the attached program file.

         Conflicts in line numbers are flagged. (See Appendix A for diagnostic messages.)
         Temporary corrections override permanent corrections. Permanent corrections
         override skeleton-generated corrections, if a conflict occurs. Since conflicts that cause
         overrides are not fatal errors, such a feature can be used to change the resulting
         output stream. When a conflict does occur, the highest priority corrections are saved
         as part of the output. Lowest priority corrections are displayed in the SSG run log.
         They follow the override message and are ignored. The reserved, global variable
         CONFLC$ can be used to monitor this (see Table 3–9).

         -N- conflicts with -N when merging corrections. If the input temporary stream looks
         like this:

          *element-name/version name
          -557-
          -557

         then the following diagnostic is produced:

          ERROR IN PROCESSING ELEMENT element-name/version-name
          SEQUENCE OR FORMAT ERROR ON FOLLOWING IMAGE
          -577

         However, if the -577- or -577 image is in the input permanent stream and the other
         image, -577 or -577-, is in the input temporary stream, a conflict results and the
         following diagnostic is issued:

          TEMP CORRECTIONS OVERRIDE PERM CORRECTIONS IN ELEMENT ;
          element-name/version-name
          -577(-)

         Where the image is listed, -577 or -577- is derived from the input permanent stream
         and does not appear in the output permanent stream.

         If -577- and -577 both appear in the input permanent stream and not in the input
         temporary stream, no error or conflict is noted and the output permanent stream is
         correct (see 5.2.30).




7831 1784–010                                                                                 5–13
SSG Directives




        Some anomalies occur when using skeleton-generated corrections. These include
        use of edit mode, continuing a correction image, and skipping images due to
        overrides. If there are no errors or overrides, skeleton-generated corrections can be
        assumed to be correct. However, you should not use edit mode to create an image as
        the results may not be correct.

        Note: When resolving line number conflicts, the WITH phrase of *MERGE
        specifies the input temporary stream that has the same priority level as if it were a
        permanent stream. In addition, where *CORRECT assumes PCF and a matching TCF
        set, *MERGE makes no such assumption. The GIVING phrase for both directives is a
        similar assumption. Use of *CORRECT,P and *MERGE directive is analogous.
        Compatibility with previous levels of SSG is retained when logic dictates.

5.2.8. *CREATE
        The *CREATE directive performs dynamic expansion of internal chains.

        Formats
          *CREATE SGS(:)       sgs-image


                                     (/   −   )
                       PERM:
          ∗
           CREATE                      −
                       TEMP:

          *CREATE SGS filename./label

        Parameters
        sgs-image

              See 3.1.1 for construction of an SGS image. Can be supplied by any combination
              of characters and, if LBRK$ is clear, bracketed references.

        element-name/version-name

              See 6.3 for construction of an element entry name. Can be supplied by any
              combination of characters and, if LBRK$ is clear, bracketed references.

        filename./label

              See 2.1.2, param-2, Format 2 for construction of this entry. Can be supplied by any
              combination of characters and, if LBRK$ is clear, bracketed references.

        Description
        After all bracketed references have been satisfied, an SGS based on sgs-image is
        created. The order in which the SGS is added to the chain of SGSs with the same label
        is determined by the value of reserved global variable SOPT$ (see 3.2.3.3). If the value
        of reserved global variable IOPT$ is nonzero, the SGS is displayed in the SSG run log,
        preceded by the skeleton line number and a colon ( : ).




5–14                                                                               7831 1784–010
                                                                                 SSG Directives




         After all bracketed references are satisfied, SSG creates an element based on
         element- name/version-name. In the absence of the O or Q options (used to alter the
         point of insertion into PCF and TCF sets, respectively) on the @SSG call, the element
         entry is normally inserted at the end of the PERM (primary PCF set) or TEMP (primary
         TCF set) chain. If /version-name is omitted, a blank version name is assumed. Once
         the new element is created, you can reference it like other elements. Created
         elements are treated as empty by SSG. The actual files used for input are not changed.

         The following format is used to associate the named file with the SGS label:

          *CREATE SGS filename./label

         The SGS file name references can then be used to reference information about the
         file. The named file does not need to be a program file. Even if filename is a standard
         program file (PF), a large program file (LPF), or a large element program file (LEPF), no
         table of contents (TOC) SGSs are created.

         If SSG is unable to assign the named file, the following message is displayed and the
         *CREATE directive is ignored:

          *ERROR* – INVALID FILE SPECIFICATION – IGNORED

         Example 1
           *CREATE SGS:     LAB [lab1,1,1,1] [label2,1,2,3]

         Example 2
           *CREATE SGS:     DEFAULT IS ...

         Example 3
           *SET IOPT TO IOPT$
           *CLEAR IOPT$
           *CREATE SGS: INTERNAL SGS IS ...
           *SET IOPT$ TO IOPT

         Example 4
           *CREATE PERM: ELT

         Example 5
           *CREATE TEMP: ELT/VER

         Example 6
           *CREATE SGS FILE./TEST




7831 1784–010                                                                                  5–15
SSG Directives




        Example 7
          *EDIT ON
          *CREATE SGS: LAB THIS,ONE,&
          SGS CREATED,USING THE &
          EDIT DIRECTIVE

        This example would create the following SGS:

         LAB THIS,ONE,SGS CREATED,USING THE EDIT DIRECTIVE

        It illustrates the use of the *EDIT directive (see 5.2.16) to create SGSs.The created
        image must not exceed the maximum number of characters defined by IMGLEN$
        (see Table 3–9).

        Example 8
          *CSF @CAT,P MYFILE(+1).,F
          *CREATE SGS MY*FILE./INFO
          *DISPLAY ‘File cycle is [INFO,0,C]’

        This example would display the absolute file cycle of the empty file created with
        the *CSF directive. It illustrates that the *CREATE directive can be used to create
        file information for files other than program files.

5.2.9. *CSF
        The *CSF directive submits Exec control statement images.

        Format
          *CSF(,options)      image

        Parameters
        options

         Can be supplied by any combination of characters. If LBRK$ is clear, bracketed
         references resolve to a string. The L option prints the CSF image in the SSG run log.
         All other specified options are ignored.

        image

         Can be supplied by any combination of characters and, if LBRK$ is clear, bracketed
         references. Leading spaces are ignored. However, the first nonspace character
         must be @.




5–16                                                                               7831 1784–010
                                                                               SSG Directives




         Description
         The *CSF directive directs SSG to perform an ER ACSF$ with the supplied image.
         Leading spaces are ignored, but the first nonspace character must be an @. (SSG shifts
         the image left so that the @ is in column 1). SSG processes image as if it is a quoted
         string, even though quotation marks are not required and should not be specified.
         SSG verifies only that the first character is @, not that the entire image is a valid ER
         ACSF$ request. Continuation is not allowed anywhere in the *CSF directive, except
         within bracketed references, if LBRK$ is clear. If a semicolon is included in the image,
         an ER (error type 04) error code 040 contingency occurs.

         Completion of an ER ACSF$ request occurs when the Exec returns control to SSG.
         Upon completion, the status is returned in register A0 by the Exec, and is provided in
         two ways. The value is available in the reserved, global variable CSFSTA$, and an
         edited value is available through the SGS reference [CSFSTA$,1,1,1] (see 3.1.4). You can
         determine the status and use this information to affect ERRCNT$, or verify
         destinations for parts of the generated output stream.

         Note: If you use *CSF to direct the SSG run log (and possibly other listings
         generated by SSG) to an alternate print file (@BRKPT), you can not assume that
         margins will be consistent. This is because SSG makes no assumptions about image
         contents.

         Example 1
           *CSF   @ASG,T FILE[*VAR].,F/1

         Example 2
           *CSF   @ADD selected-stream

         Example 3
           *CSF   @BRKPT,V    RUNSTREAM.

         Example 4
           *CSF   @BRKPT,W    DOCUMENT.UPDATE[*I]




7831 1784–010                                                                                5–17
SSG Directives




5.2.10. *DEFINE and *ENDDEFINE
        The *DEFINE directive and *ENDDEFINE directive define skeleton subroutines.

        Format
          *DEFINE define-name
          (skeleton images)
          *ENDDEFINE

        Parameter
        define-name

            Can be a string, SGS reference, or a set reference. SSG uses only the first
            20 characters. The define-name is case independent. That is, NAME is the
            same as name. Only alphanumeric, $, and _ characters are allowed. The complete
            define-name must be on the same image as the *DEFINE directive. Continuation is
            not allowed anywhere in the *DEFINE directive, not even within an SGS reference
            or set reference.

        Description
        All images between the matching *DEFINE and *ENDDEFINE directives constitute
        the define packet. None of the images are interpreted until a call for that define packet
        is made by a *PROCESS directive, or the define name is preceded by an * in column 1.
        From a define packet, nested calls for other define packets and calls for the same
        define packet are allowed to any depth. When a *PROCESS directive causes define
        packet images to be interpreted, parameters can be passed to the define packet. For
        an explanation of how to reference these parameters, see 3.3.

        Example 1
          *DEFINE     NAME

        Example 2
          *DEFINE     [NAME,1,1,1]

        Example 3
          *DEFINE     LOWER_to_upper

        Example 4
          *Define     lower_to_UPPER

        Examples 3 and 4 are equivalent.




5–18                                                                              7831 1784–010
                                                                                SSG Directives




5.2.11. *DISPLAY
         The *DISPLAY directive prints text in the SSG run log.

         Format
           *DISPLAY(,options) val (val...)

         Parameters
         options

               E   Increment the value of ERRCNT$.
               O   Display the text on the operator console.
         val

               Text displayed in the SSG run log. The parameter can be a string variable name,
               a numeric variable name, or a quoted string.

         Description
         The *DISPLAY directive prints text in the SSG run log. A space must separate each val.
         On output the strings will be concatenated, ignoring spaces unless the strings are in
         quotes or are part of a string variable value. To enclose a quoted string, use single
         quotation marks.

         Example
           *SET A = 132
           *SET MSG = ‘There are ‘
           *DISPLAY MSG A ‘ cars in the parking lot, ‘
           *DISPLAY ‘but there isn‘‘t a single yellow one. ‘

         This example prints the following string:

           There are 132 cars in the parking lot,
           but there isn‘t a single yellow one.




7831 1784–010                                                                                5–19
SSG Directives




5.2.12. *DIVIDE
        The *DIVIDE directive performs variable division.

        Format
          *DIVIDE number BY number GIVING variable-name-1(,variable-name-2)

        Parameters
        number

            Integer expression or numeric expression.

        variable-name

            Can be supplied by a string satisfying the description in 3.2.1 or by a variable
            reference, process parameter reference, SGS reference, or set reference that
            returns such a string.

        Description
        The *DIVIDE directive directs the division of the first number by the second number.
        It sets the first variable-name equal to the quotient, and the second variable-name,
        if present, equal to the remainder. The second variable-name can duplicate the first
        variable-name saving only the remainder. For each variable-name, if no local variable
        name exists, SSG searches for a global variable of that name. If no global variable
        exists, SSG creates one. Once created, this global variable exists until it is removed
        (*REMOVE).

        Note: If you attempt to divide by 0, an error diagnostic appears in the SSG run log
        and the quotient and remainder are set to 0.

        Example 1
          *DIVIDE A+B BY C-D GIVING QVO,REM

        Example 2
          *DIVIDE 1+[#P] BY 7 GIVING REM,REM

        Example 3
          *DIVIDE A BY B     GIVING    C




5–20                                                                              7831 1784–010
                                                                                   SSG Directives




5.2.13. *DLOC
         The *DLOC directive obtains or releases secure privileges.

         Format

                             
                        ON
            ∗DLOC

                        OFF

         Description
         The *DLOC directive provides the capability to obtain or release secure privileges
         while executing in SSG. These privileges can be obtained only if the user-id for the
         run in execution is authorized to obtain such privileges.

         The initial privileged state of the run is contained in [INFO$,1,4,2] and has a symbolic
         value of ON or OFF. This can be use to determine if a *DLOC directive is necessary
         (see example 1) and can be used to reset the privileged state to its initial value
         (see example 2).

         The *DLOC directive uses ER MODPS$ to modify the privileged state of the run. If
         this ER is not authorized for the run, a security error will occur. In this case, secure
         privileges may still be obtained by using the directive

          *CSF @ASG,A SYS$*DLOC$/read-key/write-key

         if the correct read key and write key for the file SYS$*DLOC$ are specified.

         Secure privileges may subsequently be released using the directive

          *CSF @FREE SYS$*DLOC$.

         Refer to 5.2.9 for additional information about the *CSF directive.

         Example 1
           *IF [INFO$,1,4,2]=OFF
           *DLOC ON
           *ENDIF

         Example 2
           *DLOC [INFO$,1,4,2]




7831 1784–010                                                                                   5–21
SSG Directives

5.2.16. *EDIT
         The *EDIT directive concatenates nondirective images.

         Format 1
         This format is used if EDITAB$ has (or will have using the C option) a value of 0.

                                 ⎡            ⎤
           ∗EDIT,      ON ⎢  .    ⎥
                                 ⎢.           ⎥
                                 ⎣                 ⎦

         Format 2
         This format is used if EDITAB$ has (or will have, using the S option) a nonzero value.

           *EDIT(,options) ON (edtsym(edtimg(edtsym(coled ... ))))



         Format 3
         This format is used to unconditionally terminate edit mode.

           *EDIT(,options) OFF




7831 1784–010                                                                                          5–23
SSG Directives




        Parameters
        options

           Can be supplied by a string or a variable reference, process parameter reference,
           SGS reference, or set reference that returns a string.

            C     Clear the value of EDITAB$ (value of 0)
            S     Set the value of EDITAB$ (value of 1).
        edtsym

           A one-character string or a variable reference, process parameter reference,
           SGS reference, or set reference that returns a string. The first character
           returned is used.

           The edit symbol (edtsym) is optional. When it is omitted, an ampersand ( & )
           is assumed.

           The period ( . ) is a valid edit symbol so comment can be supplied only when
           edtsym is specified and EDITAB$ has (or will have) a value of 0 (format 1).

           Note: It is recommended that if EDITAB$ has (or will have) a nonzero value;
           edtsym cannot be a special character.

        edtimg

           Any combination of characters and, if LBRK$ is clear, bracketed references.
           The image cannot contain the edit symbol (edtsym). Characters are edited
           until an occurrence of edtsym or end of image is encountered. End of image
           terminates edit mode and the edited image is output. An occurrence of edtsym
           initiates a search for coled. If coled is omitted, editing continues with the next
           skeleton image.

        coled

           Can be supplied by an integer or numeric expression. The value of COLED$
           (see Table 3–9) is replaced with the value of the expression. Since COLED$
           is also maintained by SSG, a “skip count” can be specified by using COLED$+
           some value.

           If edtsym terminates the scan of coled, SSG assumes another edtimg. Editing
           then continues with the current image. This new edtimg can be followed by
           edtsym and, if desired, another coled, and so on.

           If any character other than edtsym (period or space) terminates the scan of coled,
           editing continues with the next skeleton image.

           Note: The value of LBRK$ (see Table 3–9) does not affect the evaluation
           of coled.




5–24                                                                             7831 1784–010
                                                                               SSG Directives




         Description
         Following are tasks associated with the *EDIT directive:

            Building a nondirective or *CREATE SGS image
             Use edit mode to build a nondirective or a *CREATE SGS image from one or a
             group of skeleton images. Depending on the value of EDITAB$, edit mode
             operates in two ways.

                If EDITAB$ has a value of 0, the occurrence of edtsym on a nondirective
                 image terminates the scan of that image. Edit mode continues with the
                 next skeleton image.
                If EDITAB$ has a nonzero value, multiple edtsym characters are allowed on
                 the image now being edited. This allows you to specify with coled on which
                 columns to start the next edtimg. You can specify columns by placing edtsym
                 on the *EDIT directive or on a nondirective image.

                 The edited image starts with the first edtimg. The first coled is evaluated
                 and used to set the value of COLED$ (specify a column position). The next
                 edtimg is edited, the next coled is evaluated, and so on.

            Turning on edit mode
             Turn on edit mode with the *EDIT ON directive. The initial image is space-filled
             and the value of COLED$ is set to 1.

                When edit mode is on, all nondirective or *CREATE images terminated by
                 edtsym are concatenated. The value of COLED$ is increased as the image
                 is built.

                If the image being edited becomes larger than the number of characters
                 defined by the value of IMGLEN$ (see 4.3), a semicolon is automatically
                 inserted and the image is output. Editing then begins with a new space-
                 filled image and the value of COLED$ is set to 1.

                 Note: An edit-mode *CREATE image cannot exceed the maximum number
                 of characters defined by the value of IMGLEN$.

            Execution when the edit mode is on
             You can execute directive images (other than *BRKPT, *CORRECT, and
             *MERGE) while you edit.

                Any nondirective image without edtsym terminates edit mode.

                The *EDIT OFF directive terminates edit mode.

                The final created image is output to the generated output stream or
                 the PCFzzzz specified on a *CORRECT directive, or both.




7831 1784–010                                                                               5–25
SSG Directives




                SSG ignores any *EDIT OFF directive outside of edit mode. The value of
                 COLED$ is set to 0.

                An *EDIT ON directive encountered in edit mode destroys partially edited
                 images and repositions the column pointer, COLED$, to column 1.

            Editing a *CREATE image
             When you edit a *CREATE image, it must follow the *EDIT ON directive.

                You can execute any other directive image (other than *BRKPT, *CORRECT,
                 and *MERGE) while edit mode is on, but the occurrence of any portion of the
                 *CREATE image without edtsym terminates edit mode.

                You can terminate edit mode with the *EDIT OFF directive.

                The final image is added to the chain of *CREATE images.

            Modifying COLED$ and EDITAB$
             You can use a global variable modifying directive to modify the values of COLED$
             and EDITAB$.

                You can control image construction and type using an edit image. Changes
                 to COLED$ are not recognized until the next nondirective image or edtimg
                 is processed, and EDITAB$ has a nonzero value (see 4.3).

                If EDITAB$ has a nonzero value and the first character in the first edtimg
                 specified on the *EDIT directive is “ or #, the conversion based on the value
                 of PILO$ or NSIGN$ is performed.

                For other (nondirective) images, the character in the first column position is
                 checked.

        Example 1
            *EDIT ON825

        Example 2
            *EDIT OFF

        Example 3
            *EDIT ON &A&11&b&21&C

        Example 4
            *EDIT ON &LAB&11&L,U&21&A0,[#1]&39&.

        Example 5
            *EDIT,S ON &
            LAB&11
            L,U&21
            A0,X1&39



5–26                                                                               7831 1784–010
                                                                               SSG Directives




         Example 6
           *EDIT,S ON &
           LAB&11&L,U&21
           A0,X1&39&. This is a &
           COMMENT

5.2.17. *EJECT
         The *EJECT directive controls page ejects.

         Format
           *EJECT(,options)

         Parameter
          options

             Can be supplied by a string or an SGS or set reference that returns a string.
             Valid values are

            none      Assumes the E option.
            D         When the revised skeleton stream is listed by using the E option
                      in combination with the D option on the @SSG call (see Table 2–1),
                      a page eject occurs. That is, the next image of the revised
                      skeleton stream, if it exists, appears on the next page.
            E         When the revised skeleton stream is listed by using the E option
                      without the D option on the @SSG call (see Table 2–1), a page
                      eject occurs. That is, the next image of the revised skeleton
                      stream, if it exists, appears on the next page.
            K         When the generated output stream is listed by using the K option
                      on the @SSG call (see Table 2–1) or an *BRKPT directive (see
                      5.2.4), a page eject occurs. That is, the next image of the
                      generated output stream, if it exists, appears on the next page.


         Description
         The *EJECT directive makes the revised skeleton stream and generated output
         streams easier to read. It separates logically independent sections of output. The E, D,
         and K options can appear on the same *EJECT directive, in any order.

         Note: The options specification on an *EJECT directive is determined at the time
         the revised skeleton stream is built and before it is processed. You cannot use a
         process parameter reference to supply options, and cannot modify the options
         during processing.




7831 1784–010                                                                                5–27
SSG Directives




5.2.18. *ELSE
        The *ELSE directive specifies the start of the block of code to be executed if the *IF
        condition is not true.

5.2.19. *ELSEIF
        The *ELSEIF directive has the same affect as an *ELSE followed immediately by an *IF.

5.2.20. *ENDCORRECT
        The *ENDCORRECT signifies the end of a *CORRECT block.

5.2.21. *ENDDEFINE (,E)
        The *ENDDEFINE directive signifies the end of a *DEFINE block. The E option causes
        a page eject following the *ENDDEFINE if you did not use the D option on the SSG
        processor call.

5.2.22. *ENDDO (,E)
        The *ENDDO directive signifies the end of a *DO block. The E option causes a page
        eject following the *ENDDO if you did not use the D option on the SSG processor call.

5.2.23. *ENDIF (,E)
        The *ENDIF signifies the end of an *IF, *ELSE, or *ELSEIF block. The E option causes
        a page eject following the *ENDIF if you did not use the D option on the SSG
        processor call.

5.2.24. *EXIT
        The *EXIT directive allows early termination of a loop.

        Format
            *EXIT   (varname)

        Description
        The exit directive terminates an *INCREMENT or *DO loop.

             If varname is not specified, the innermost loop is terminated.
             If varname is specified, the loop using that loop control variable is terminated.
             If varname does not exist, a NO FIND occurs and the directive is ignored.




5–28                                                                                 7831 1784–010
                                                                                SSG Directives




         Example
             *INCREMENT COUNT TO [PARK]
               *IF [PARK,COUNT,1,1]=STOP
                 *EXIT
               *ENDIF
               *CSF @ASG,T [PARK,COUNT,1,1]
             *LOOP

5.2.25. *IF, *ELSEIF, *ELSE, and *ENDIF
         *IF and *ELSEIF are decision-making directives designed to test (evaluate) conditions
         and choose logical paths in the skeleton. This is accomplished by either interpreting or
         skipping a block of skeleton images. There are four general formats for *IF in a
         skeleton.

         Format 1
             *IF condition
             (skeleton images)
             *ENDIF

         You can put any directive or nondirective skeleton image except *DEFINE between the
         matching *IF and *ENDIF directives.

             If condition is true, images between the *IF and *ENDIF directives are interpreted.
             If condition is false, they are skipped.
         Format 2
             *IF condition
             (skeleton images)
             *ELSE
             (skeleton images)
             *ENDIF

         You can put any directive or nondirective skeleton image except *DEFINE between
         the matching *IF and *ELSE and the matching *ELSE and *ENDIF directives.

             If condition is true, the skeleton images between the *IF and *ELSE directives are
              interpreted, and the images between the *ELSE and *ENDIF directives are skipped.
             If condition is false, the skeleton images between the *IF and *ELSE directives are
              skipped and the images between the *ELSE and *ENDIF directives are interpreted.




7831 1784–010                                                                               5–29
SSG Directives




        Format 3
            *IF condition-1
            (skeleton images)
            *ELSEIF condition-2
            (skeleton images)
                  .
                  .
                  .

            *ELSEIF condition-n
            (skeleton images)
            *ENDIF

        You can put any directive or nondirective skeleton image except *DEFINE between
        the matching *IF and *ELSEIF, *ELSEIF and *ELSEIF, ..., and *ELSEIF and *ENDIF
        directives.

            If condition-1 is true, the skeleton images between the *IF directive and first
             *ELSEIF directive are interpreted. All other images are skipped. If condition-1
             is false, condition-2 is evaluated.
            If condition-2 is true, images between the first and second *ELSEIF directives
             are interpreted. All other images are skipped. If condition-2 is false, the next
             condition-i is evaluated until a condition-i is true or there is no further condition-i
             to evaluate.
            If no condition-i is true, no images are interpreted.


        Note: The *ELSF and *END directives are still valid, but their use is discouraged
        in new skeletons. The *ELSEIF and *ENDIF directives should be used instead.

        Format 4
            *IF condition-1
            (skeleton images)
            *ELSEIF condition-2
            (skeleton images)
                  .
                  .
                  .

            *ELSEIF condition-n
            (skeleton images)
            *ELSE
            (skeleton images)
            *ENDIF




5–30                                                                                  7831 1784–010
                                                                                    SSG Directives




         You can put any directive or nondirective skeleton image except *DEFINE between
         the matching *IF and *ELSEIF, *ELSEIF and *ELSEIF, ..., *ELSEIF and *ELSE, and *ELSE
         and *ENDIF directives.

              If condition-1 is true, the skeleton images between the *IF directive and first
               *ELSEIF directive are interpreted. All other images are skipped. If condition-1
               is false, condition-2 is evaluated.
              If condition-2 is true, images between the first and second *ELSEIF directives are
               interpreted. All other images are skipped. If condition-2 is false, the next
               condition-i is evaluated until a condition-i is true or there is no further condition-i
               to evaluate.
              If no condition-i is true, images between the *ELSE and *ENDIF directives are
               interpreted.

         An *ELSE or *ELSEIF directive is assumed to match the last *IF directive that has not
         been matched with an *ENDIF or an *ELSE and *ENDIF directive. A matched set of
         *IF, *ELSEIF, *ELSE, and *ENDIF directives is an IF packet. The IF packet shown in
         Format 4 can be nested statically or dynamically to any level. No overlap of IF packets
         with other IF packets, or with increment loops, is allowed. Each increment loop or IF
         packet must be nested entirely within another packet or loop (unless it starts the
         nest). Verification that *IF directives and increment loops are properly nested and that
         *IF, *ELSEIF, *ELSE, and *ENDIF directives are correctly matched is performed when
         you build the revised skeleton.

                *LOOP                                                  *ELSE
                                                                        ------

                  ------

                *ENDIF                                                ------

             *ENDIF                                                   ------

                                                                      ------
                                                                      ------
                                                                 *ENDIF
